The first one is about me so I would put this picture something with your face where you share your story why we can trust your opinion and why you started this account in the first place. Second one is restaurants so best meals brunch spot hidden gem that only locals know. Next one is experiences so activities rooftops event and let people know if they need to book something in advance. So here you save all the cool events. Then ideally you want to do one day plan so write a whole day plan from like 8 a.m till 10 p.m so what people can do in Chicago for one day. The last one is free maps so this is where you ask people to reply with a keyword then you ask them to follow before they get the Google map. So that's how you're going to grow your community. And the last one is six ways to get money from this account but be careful. This is where most coaches and creators make mistakes. They sell only low-ticket products. First one is digital products. This is the easiest and this is where you sell personalized itineraries. For example like twenty hundred dollars euros depending on how many days. Then pre-made guides so you just pack all the places that you know in one guide. For example weekend in Chicago, food guide, hidden gems. So it's not just a free map but actually guides with description and something useful. Next one is brand deals so this is where restaurants and hotels actually pay you to go and like stay for a night or have a free meal. You leave a review or you tag them in your account. Next one is affiliate income so it's like different platforms apps or even those individual restaurants or hotels can give you a discount code that you can give to your followers and whenever they use it you get a percentage of the sale. Next one is paid newsletters so this is where you share different events that take place this week or next month. So people can reserve them in advance or they never heard about them. And last one is services. This is the most profitable so this is something where you create group trips, create experiences where you organize an event. For example fun Friday night for women and you create a group of 10 and you just show them the best five bars in the city. Or a completely different experience is you can do a wellness Saturday where you find a good yoga studio and then take them to the best brunch place in town. It makes you refine your thinking because there is a thousand, there's a million different types of ways to make $10,000 per month, but if you wanna make, if you wanna 10X your company in the next three years, it refines your thinking a lot because there's not a lot of ways that you can do that, and so you have to really brainstorm and reverse engineer the thought experiment of how could that be accomplished, and typically it comes down to a handful of action items and long-term projects and investments that you need to make, but it gives you a lot of clarity, and if you have the belief to back it up, it makes it very highly likely that you can achieve those goals.

### https://www.instagram.com/reel/DXpc3w2gm3G/
- **Metrics:** 1,246 views | 9 likes | 1 shares | 0 comments | 0 saves
- **Transcription:** Serena Williams has an 80% win rate in Grand Slam finals. The bigger the moment, the better she played. Not despite the pressure, but because of it. Psychologists call this stress inoculation, the practice of deliberately reframing high-pressure situations as performance signals rather than threat signals. Most creators feel pressure and interpret it as a stop sign. The launch didn't go perfectly, the video underperformed, the pitch fell flat, and the pressure of that moment becomes a reason to pull back. Serena felt the same physiological response in a Wimbledon final that you feel hitting publish on something that matters. Elevated heart rate, heightened awareness, sharpened focus. The difference is the interpretation. She trained herself to read those signals to leap forward rather than step backward. The next time you feel resistance before posting something that actually means something to you, a bold opinion, a vulnerable story, a direct pitch, recognize that feeling as signal to move forward, not to step back. That discomfort is your nervous system telling you this one matters, that it has stakes, that it's worth something. The content that makes you hesitate the longest is usually the content your audience needs the most. Serena didn't become the greatest by stepping back when there was pressure, but rather sprinting forward.
